The brushes, the clay. It doesn’t have that spark it used to. I’m sorry, I know I’ve said that already, but that’s really the best way I can describe it.

But then when I’m there, I kind of just… sit and look at everything. The clay, the brushes, whatever it is. It doesn’t really catch for me the way it used to. I keep hoping the spark will come back once I start. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… but not the way it used to. I’ll still set out my paints, or I’ll think, “I should go to pottery class, that usually helps,” but then I get there and I just sort of stare at the brushes or the clay. I don’t feel that little spark I used to. I’m trying, though. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really in the same way. I still try to set out my paints, and I think about going to pottery class because I used to look forward to that. But when I’m actually there, I mostly just stare at the brushes or the clay and it doesn’t… it doesn’t light up for me like it used to. I’m still trying, though.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about six months, give or take. I kept telling myself it was just a rough patch and that my usual things would start helping again, like art class or my morning walks, but they haven’t really felt restorative for quite a while now. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I still try with the things I used to enjoy — I’ll set out my paints, or I’ll tell myself to go to pottery class — but then I’m there and I kind of just… stare at everything. The brushes, the clay. It doesn’t have that spark it used to. I’m sorry, I know I’ve said that already, but that’s really the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt better in the past six months?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… or at least I thought I was doing better before this slump really settled in. I had a stretch where I felt very sure of myself — maybe a little too sure — and I wasn’t as patient as I usually try to be. I’d snap at people over small comments, especially when my district manager questioned my store layouts. \n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but… it was noticeable. Does that count as “feeling better,” or do you mean more steady better?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about that period where you felt very sure of yourself and weren't as patient as usual?\n\nPatient: I mean, it wasn’t anything dramatic, I don’t think… I just felt unusually confident, like I really knew best, especially at work. And if someone made even a small comment, I’d get irritated much faster than I normally would.\n\nWith my district manager, when she questioned one of my store layouts, I’d sort of… take it personally and snap back. Not yelling or anything like that, but sharper than I’d like to be. I remember thinking, “Why are you second-guessing me?” over things that probably weren’t that big. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did this period where you felt very sure of yourself and snapped at people happen at the same time as these thoughts about not waking up and feeling down, or were they at different times?\n\nPatient: I think they were mostly part of the same general stretch, if that makes sense… not all at the exact same moment, but this whole up-and-down thing with my mood and energy has been going on for about two months now.\n\nIt seemed to start around when the trouble with the new district manager really began. I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t quite myself.\n\nDoctor: Did this period where you felt very sure of yourself and snapped at people happen before you started feeling down and having thoughts about not waking up, or did it happen after those feelings started?\n\nPatient: It was before the slump really hit, I think. I had that stretch where I felt almost… too sure of myself, like I knew exactly what I was doing, and I’d get snippy over little comments — especially when my district manager questioned my layouts.\n\nThen after that, or maybe as that settled down, the lower feelings became more noticeable. I’m trying to get the order right, but yes, the “too sure of myself” part came first.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were getting enough sleep during that time when you felt very sure of yourself and were snapping at people?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure, honestly. I don’t remember thinking, “I’m not sleeping,” exactly. I may have been a little keyed up, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it was.\n\nI think I was still going to bed and functioning, so… I guess I felt like I was getting enough? At least I didn’t notice anything really obvious with my sleep during that part. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your irritability and snapping at people during that time when you felt very sure of yourself was part of the same feelings of being down, or was it a separate experience?\n\nPatient: I think it was a separate experience, or at least it felt different from the feeling down part.\n\nBefore this slump really hit, I had this stretch where I felt almost too sure of myself, and I’d snap at people over tiny comments. It was usually when my district manager questioned my layouts. I feel awful saying that, and I don’t mean I want to hurt myself. I don’t want to do that. It’s more… the thought has been there, and that made me think I should probably talk to someone. Does that make sense?

I haven’t been watching the scale very closely, though—should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I wouldn’t call myself anxious exactly. Maybe a little keyed up at times, but nothing dramatic. I can still get through work and everything. Do you mean like worrying constantly, or more like feeling physically tense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I mean, nothing terrible, I don’t think. But I do find myself going over small things at work more than I probably should. I know that sounds a bit dramatic. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I do still try to do the things I’m supposed to enjoy… like I’ll set out my paints, or I’ll tell myself I should go to pottery class because it’s good for me. But then when I’m there, I kind of just… sit and look at everything. The clay, the brushes, whatever it is. It doesn’t really catch for me the way it used to. I don’t feel that little spark I used to. I’m trying, though. I’m still trying, though.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about six months, give or take. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really like this, no. I mean, I’ve had periods where I felt down or tired of things, but this low, kind of flat feeling has been going on for about six months now.

I kept thinking it was just a phase or that I should be able to shake it off, but even things that usually help me—like my art classes or morning walks—stopped feeling like they were restoring me. I don’t know if that counts as an “episode,” but it’s been longer than I’m comfortable with.
